{"parentUuid":"8fcadc07-fcae-4a1e-abe1-8bb6b5423f5d","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfkJbN4UzGR516ZxKBfi4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01ABn2DpLQVZGkzi7mS1nuPG","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/notes/token-budget-plan.md","content":"# Token budget plan\n\nManual Part 5.4. Written 2026-09-29 for Stage 1. Updated 2026-10-05 with\nStage 2 (T12–T24) and the wrap-up. The \"Estimated\" column is what I expected\ngoing in. The \"Actual\" columns come from `notes/usage-ledger.csv`.\n\n## How the actuals are measured\n\nEach row of the ledger is one turn, with the 5-hour and weekly meters as the\nstatus line read them. A turn's cost is the rise from the reading before it,\nin time order across the whole ledger. A drop means a window reset, and\ncounts as zero. Gaps of more than two hours are not counted, because usage\noutside this project falls into them.\n\n`scripts/usage_report.py` takes the deltas only between rows of the same\nphase. That works for a ticket that runs once. It overcounts a phase that\ncomes back days later, because it charges the gap to that phase. Its\n`spec` row says 87% of a 5-hour window, but the measured cost was 9%.\nIts `T24` row says 24% of the week, but 21 points of that are the gap from\nOct 2 to Oct 5. The figures below use the time-ordered method.\n\nBoth meters move in whole percents. A single ticket's weekly figure is\naccurate only to ±1.\n\n## Models and effort, as actually used\n\nThe manual's default puts Sonnet on `/implement`. I ran every ticket on Opus.\n\n| Stage | Model | Effort |\n|---|---|---|\n| Stage 1 `/grill-with-docs`, `/to-spec`, `/to-tickets` | Sonnet 5 | medium |\n| Stage 2 grill, spec, and tickets (specs #14, #22, #25) | Opus 5.5 | high |\n| `/implement` + `/tdd`, T01–T24 | Opus 5.5 | high (two T02 turns at medium) |\n| `/code-review` (sub-agents, same session as each ticket) | Opus 5.5 | high |\n| `/research`, quick lookups | none run as a separate stage | — |\n\n## Estimate vs. actual\n\nThe estimates are in % of the **weekly** cap. That is how I thought about it.\nThe 5-hour column shows actuals only. I made no new estimate for Stage 2, so\nthe Stage 1 per-ticket estimate stands for both stages.\n\n| Stage | Estimated (weekly) | Actual (weekly) | Actual (5-hour) |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Stage 1 planning (grill + spec + tickets) | ~2% | 1% | 6% |\n| Stage 1 tickets, T01–T11, per ticket | ~2% | 1.1% average (0–2% each) | 8.6% average (4–14%) |\n| Spec #11 tickets (T10, T11) | — | 1% | 2% |\n| Stage 1 submission + feedback | no estimate | 2% | 19% |\n| Stage 2 planning, three specs | ~2% per spec | 3% in all | 20% in all |\n| Stage 2 tickets, T12–T24, per ticket | ~2% | 3.2% average (2–5% each) | 23.8% average (14–35%) |\n| Stage 2 wrap-up (session 29, after T24) | no estimate | 3% | 27% |\n\n`/code-review` ran inside each ticket's own session, so the ledger can't split\nit from `/implement`.\n\nStage 1 tickets came in under the estimate. Stage 2 tickets came in about\n60% over it. The costliest were the Editor (T13, 4%), the Chart builder (T17,\n5%), and the homepage, summaries, and search (T20, 4%). From T16 on, each\nticket also had a browser check with screenshots.\n\n| Ticket | Weekly | 5-hour | | Ticket | Weekly | 5-hour |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| T12 | 3% | 20% | | T19 | 2% | 16% |\n| T13 | 4% | 35% | | T20 | 4% | 31% |\n| T14 | 4% | 26% | | T21 | 2% | 22% |\n| T15 | 2% | 15% | | T22 | 4% | 20% |\n| T16 | 4% | 31% | | T23 | 3% | 24% |\n| T17 | 5% | 35% | | T24 | 2% | 14% |\n| T18 | 3% | 21% | | | | |\n\n## The questions in 5.4\n\n**How many 5-hour windows?** Seven, two for Stage 1 and five for Stage 2.\n\n| Window starts (UTC) | Meter | Work |\n|---|---|---|\n| Sep 28 | 0 → 50% | Stage 1 planning, T01–T05 |\n| Sep 29 | 7 → 79% | T06–T11, Stage 1 submission |\n| Sep 30, 19:09 | 1 → 100% | Stage 2 planning (spec #14), T12–T15 |\n| Oct 1, 00:04 | 0 → 9% | end of T15, start of T16 |\n| Oct 1, 13:47 | 2 → 91% | T16–T18, spec #22 and its tickets |\n| Oct 1, 17:49 | 4 → 99% | T19, T20, spec #25's tickets, T21, T22 |\n| Oct 1, 22:41 | 4 → 69% | end of T22, T23, T24, wrap-up |\n\nStage 1 never filled a window. Stage 2 filled one and nearly filled another:\n\n- T15 reached 100% at 23:26 on Sep 30. Its last turns logged 100% until the\n  window rolled over at about 00:00.\n- T22 reached 99% at 20:53 on Oct 1. The next turn was at 22:41, in a new\n  window.\n\nThe 17:49 window began less than five hours after the 13:47 one. The weekly\nmeter reset at the same moment, from 12% to 1%.\n\n**How much of the week?**\n\n- Estimated: 2% + 24 tickets × 2% + 2% per later spec ≈ 54%.\n- Actual: 64 weekly points in all, over three cycles of the weekly meter:\n  - Sep 28 – Oct 1 at 00:19: 1 → 33%. This was all of Stage 1, plus spec #14\n    and T12–T15.\n  - Oct 1, 13:47 – 17:17: 0 → 12%.\n  - Oct 1 at 17:49 – Oct 2: 1 → 22%.\n\n  Stage 1 used 17 points and Stage 2 used 48. No single weekly cycle went\n  past 33%.\n\n**Context:** the peak was 40%, in session `9020c1e3` while cutting spec #25\ninto tickets. There was one compaction, in session 29's wrap-up (see\n`docs/process/compaction-log.md`).\n\n**What I'd cut first if over budget:** effort, from high to medium, keeping\nOpus. That cut was never made. When the 5-hour window ran out in T15 and\nT22, the work waited for the window to reset instead.\n\n## Re-forecast\n\nNo tickets remain. What is left is the report, the AI Use Statement, the\nissue comments, and the `mp2-final` tag. I write those myself, so they need\nlittle model time.\n\nThis session started on Oct 5 with the weekly meter at 43% and the 5-hour\nmeter at 1%. The 21 points between Oct 2 and Oct 5 aren't in the ledger.\nNothing in this repo was committed in that time, so I don't count them\ntoward this project.\n\nEven at the Stage 2 wrap-up's cost (3% of the week, 27% of a window), the\nrest fits easily in the 57% of the week that is left.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1911,"cache_read_input_tokens":78170,"output_tokens":2872,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":197},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1911,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":2872,"cache_read_input_tokens":78170,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1911,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1911},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_01ABn2DpLQVZGkzi7mS1nuPG":{"file_path":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/notes/token-budget-plan.md","content":"# Token budget plan\n\nManual Part 5.4. Written 2026-09-29 for Stage 1. Updated 2026-10-05 with\nStage 2 (T12–T24) and the wrap-up. The \"Estimated\" column is what I expected\ngoing in. The \"Actual\" columns come from `notes/usage-ledger.csv`.\n\n## How the actuals are measured\n\nEach row of the ledger is one turn, with the 5-hour and weekly meters as the\nstatus line read them. A turn's cost is the rise from the reading before it,\nin time order across the whole ledger. A drop means a window reset, and\ncounts as zero. Gaps of more than two hours are not counted, because usage\noutside this project falls into them.\n\n`scripts/usage_report.py` takes the deltas only between rows of the same\nphase. That works for a ticket that runs once. It overcounts a phase that\ncomes back days later, because it charges the gap to that phase. Its\n`spec` row says 87% of a 5-hour window, but the measured cost was 9%.\nIts `T24` row says 24% of the week, but 21 points of that are the gap from\nOct 2 to Oct 5. The figures below use the time-ordered method.\n\nBoth meters move in whole percents. A single ticket's weekly figure is\naccurate only to ±1.\n\n## Models and effort, as actually used\n\nThe manual's default puts Sonnet on `/implement`. I ran every ticket on Opus.\n\n| Stage | Model | Effort |\n|---|---|---|\n| Stage 1 `/grill-with-docs`, `/to-spec`, `/to-tickets` | Sonnet 5 | medium |\n| Stage 2 grill, spec, and tickets (specs #14, #22, #25) | Opus 5.5 | high |\n| `/implement` + `/tdd`, T01–T24 | Opus 5.5 | high (two T02 turns at medium) |\n| `/code-review` (sub-agents, same session as each ticket) | Opus 5.5 | high |\n| `/research`, quick lookups | none run as a separate stage | — |\n\n## Estimate vs. actual\n\nThe estimates are in % of the **weekly** cap. That is how I thought about it.\nThe 5-hour column shows actuals only. I made no new estimate for Stage 2, so\nthe Stage 1 per-ticket estimate stands for both stages.\n\n| Stage | Estimated (weekly) | Actual (weekly) | Actual (5-hour) |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Stage 1 planning (grill + spec + tickets) | ~2% | 1% | 6% |\n| Stage 1 tickets, T01–T11, per ticket | ~2% | 1.1% average (0–2% each) | 8.6% average (4–14%) |\n| Spec #11 tickets (T10, T11) | — | 1% | 2% |\n| Stage 1 submission + feedback | no estimate | 2% | 19% |\n| Stage 2 planning, three specs | ~2% per spec | 3% in all | 20% in all |\n| Stage 2 tickets, T12–T24, per ticket | ~2% | 3.2% average (2–5% each) | 23.8% average (14–35%) |\n| Stage 2 wrap-up (session 29, after T24) | no estimate | 3% | 27% |\n\n`/code-review` ran inside each ticket's own session, so the ledger can't split\nit from `/implement`.\n\nStage 1 tickets came in under the estimate. Stage 2 tickets came in about\n60% over it. The costliest were the Editor (T13, 4%), the Chart builder (T17,\n5%), and the homepage, summaries, and search (T20, 4%). From T16 on, each\nticket also had a browser check with screenshots.\n\n| Ticket | Weekly | 5-hour | | Ticket | Weekly | 5-hour |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| T12 | 3% | 20% | | T19 | 2% | 16% |\n| T13 | 4% | 35% | | T20 | 4% | 31% |\n| T14 | 4% | 26% | | T21 | 2% | 22% |\n| T15 | 2% | 15% | | T22 | 4% | 20% |\n| T16 | 4% | 31% | | T23 | 3% | 24% |\n| T17 | 5% | 35% | | T24 | 2% | 14% |\n| T18 | 3% | 21% | | | | |\n\n## The questions in 5.4\n\n**How many 5-hour windows?** Seven, two for Stage 1 and five for Stage 2.\n\n| Window starts (UTC) | Meter | Work |\n|---|---|---|\n| Sep 28 | 0 → 50% | Stage 1 planning, T01–T05 |\n| Sep 29 | 7 → 79% | T06–T11, Stage 1 submission |\n| Sep 30, 19:09 | 1 → 100% | Stage 2 planning (spec #14), T12–T15 |\n| Oct 1, 00:04 | 0 → 9% | end of T15, start of T16 |\n| Oct 1, 13:47 | 2 → 91% | T16–T18, spec #22 and its tickets |\n| Oct 1, 17:49 | 4 → 99% | T19, T20, spec #25's tickets, T21, T22 |\n| Oct 1, 22:41 | 4 → 69% | end of T22, T23, T24, wrap-up |\n\nStage 1 never filled a window. Stage 2 filled one and nearly filled another:\n\n- T15 reached 100% at 23:26 on Sep 30. Its last turns logged 100% until the\n  window rolled over at about 00:00.\n- T22 reached 99% at 20:53 on Oct 1. The next turn was at 22:41, in a new\n  window.\n\nThe 17:49 window began less than five hours after the 13:47 one. The weekly\nmeter reset at the same moment, from 12% to 1%.\n\n**How much of the week?**\n\n- Estimated: 2% + 24 tickets × 2% + 2% per later spec ≈ 54%.\n- Actual: 64 weekly points in all, over three cycles of the weekly meter:\n  - Sep 28 – Oct 1 at 00:19: 1 → 33%. This was all of Stage 1, plus spec #14\n    and T12–T15.\n  - Oct 1, 13:47 – 17:17: 0 → 12%.\n  - Oct 1 at 17:49 – Oct 2: 1 → 22%.\n\n  Stage 1 used 17 points and Stage 2 used 48. No single weekly cycle went\n  past 33%.\n\n**Context:** the peak was 40%, in session `9020c1e3` while cutting spec #25\ninto tickets. There was one compaction, in session 29's wrap-up (see\n`docs/process/compaction-log.md`).\n\n**What I'd cut first if over budget:** effort, from high to medium, keeping\nOpus. That cut was never made. When the 5-hour window ran out in T15 and\nT22, the work waited for the window to reset instead.\n\n## Re-forecast\n\nNo tickets remain. What is left is the report, the AI Use Statement, the\nissue comments, and the `mp2-final` tag. I write those myself, so they need\nlittle model time.\n\nThis session started on Oct 5 with the weekly meter at 43% and the 5-hour\nmeter at 1%. The 21 points between Oct 2 and Oct 5 aren't in the ledger.\nNothing in this repo was committed in that time, so I don't count them\ntoward this project.\n\nEven at the Stage 2 wrap-up's cost (3% of the week, 27% of a window), the\nrest fits easily in the 57% of the week that is left.\n"}},"serverClassifierRequest":"66d0dea8-55b9-4233-8229-7a4ae8ebc5b2","apiBlockIndex":2,"requestId":"req_011CfkJbMnNU2v9npMM8a5Jy","type":"assistant","uuid":"96b9cd02-176e-419e-b8f5-3198bc2703c2","timestamp":"2026-10-06T03:52:04.514Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"high","perTurnEffort":"high","session_id":"d5c3385e-b66b-4027-9837-a0bfd3de9dbc","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"ac996459-8cb0-425f-b2f6-d5e77314800b","version":"2.1.285","gitBranch":"main"}

The 21 points between Oct 2 and Oct 5 aren't in the ledger.","+Nothing in this repo was committed in that time, so I don't count them","+toward this project.","+","+Even at the Stage 2 wrap-up's cost (3% of the week, 27% of a window), the","+rest fits easily in the 57% of the week that is left."]}],"originalFile":"# Token budget plan\n\nManual Part 5.4. Written 2026-09-29. The \"Estimated\" column is what I expected\ngoing in; the \"Actual\" columns come from `notes/usage-ledger.csv` through\n`uv run python scripts/usage_report.py`.\n\n## Models and effort, as actually used\n\nThe manual's default puts Sonnet on `/implement`. I ran every ticket on Opus.\n\n| Stage | Model | Effort |\n|---|---|---|\n| `/grill-with-docs`, `/to-spec`, `/to-tickets` | Sonnet 5 | medium |\n| `/implement` + `/tdd`, T01–T11 | Opus 5.5 | high (one T02 turn at medium) |\n| `/code-review` (sub-agents, same session as each ticket) | Opus 5.5 | high |\n| `/research`, quick lookups | none run as a separate stage | — |\n\n## Estimate vs. actual\n\nThe estimates are in % of the **weekly** cap; that is how I thought about it.\nThe 5-hour column is actual only.\n\n| Stage | Estimated (weekly) | Actual (weekly) | Actual (5-hour window) |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Grill + spec + tickets, combined | ~2% | 1.0% | 6.0% |\n| `/implement` + `/tdd`, per ticket | ~1% | 0.8% on average, review included (0–1% each) | 3.0–6.0% each |\n| `/code-review`, per ticket | ~1% | counted in the row above: it ran in the same session | — |\n| Lookups | no estimate | none logged separately | — |\n\n`/code-review` ran inside each ticket's own session, so the ledger can't split\nit from `/implement`. Together I estimated about 2% of the week per ticket.\nThe measured cost was 0.8%.\n\n## The questions in 5.4\n\n**How many 5-hour windows?** Two, for the planning session and 11 tickets\n(the original 9 plus T10 and T11):\n\n- Sep 28: 0% → 50% of one window (planning, T01–T05).\n- Sep 29: 7% → 60% of a second window (T06–T11).\n\nNeither window filled.\n\n**How much of the week?**\n\n- Estimated: about 2% + 11 × 2% ≈ 24%.\n- Actual: the weekly meter went from 1% to 16%, so about 15% of the week.\n\n**What I'd cut first if over budget:** effort, from high to medium, keeping\nOpus.\n\n## Re-forecast\n\n`usage_report.py` puts the average at 0.8% of the weekly cap per ticket, and\n16% of the week is used. No core tickets remain. The weekly meter leaves room\nfor stretch tickets.\n","userModified":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"96b9cd02-176e-419e-b8f5-3198bc2703c2","session_id":"d5c3385e-b66b-4027-9837-a0bfd3de9dbc","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"ac996459-8cb0-425f-b2f6-d5e77314800b","version":"2.1.285","gitBranch":"main"}

It now covers the whole project, Stage 2 (T12–T24) and the wrap-up included, not just T01–T11.\n\n**What the ledger shows:**\n- **Per ticket:** Stage 1 tickets averaged 1.1% of the weekly cap, under the ~2% estimate. Stage 2 tickets averaged 3.2%, about 60% over it. The most expensive were the Editor (T13, 4%), the Chart builder (T17, 5%) and the homepage and search (T20, 4%).\n- **Weekly total:** 64 points in all (16 in Stage 1, 48 in Stage 2), against an estimate of about 56%.\n- **5-hour windows:** seven in total. Twice the window ran out:\n  - T15 hit 100% on Sep 30.\n  - T22 hit 99% on Oct 1, and the next turn came about 1h48m later, in a new window.\n\n  Neither time was effort lowered; the work waited for the window to reset.\n- **Model change:** Stage 2 planning ran on Opus 5.5 at high effort, not Sonnet. The plan now says so.\n\n**Problems I found in the data:**\n- **The numbers from `scripts/usage_report.py` were inflated.** It works out costs one phase at a time, so a phase that comes back days later gets the whole gap charged to it. It reported `spec` at 87% of a 5-hour window when the real cost was 9%, and T24 at 24% of the week when it was 2%. I recomputed everything from consecutive readings in time order, leaving out any gap over two hours, and the plan explains this method. I haven't changed the script itself.\n- **This session was logged as T24** because `.claude/state/phase` still said T24. I set it to `submission`. The one row already written still says T24.\n- **The weekly meter reset twice on Oct 1,** dropping from 12% to 1% at about 17:30 UTC along with the 5-hour meter. The plan reports this as observed and doesn't guess at the cause.\n- **21 weekly points between Oct 2 and Oct 5 aren't in the ledger.** Nothing was committed to this repo in that time, so the plan doesn't count them toward the project.\n\nI haven't committed anything. While I was working, `README.md` was edited and a report file appeared in `docs/`, so I left those alone. {"parentUuid":"a31685b1-768c-41fc-9e37-63c48649733b","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"fe236e3e-9950-49ac-b05b-49c9855d25c0","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018r3Rehr6zp4WXyuaDf18qp","type":"tool_result","content":"## Parent\n\nPart of #11\n\n## What to build\n\nA Data Bite can carry chart images (PNG, JPEG, WebP), uploaded and deleted on its edit page, and shown inline wherever its Markdown body references them as `image:<name>`: the admin preview, its public page, and the `cms publish` export. This ticket builds the shared image code (validation, storage, the `image:` rewrite in the Markdown pipeline, the public and export paths) that T11 reuses for Reports.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] Any Analyst or the Director can upload an image to any Data Bite, draft or published; the edit page lists its images, each with a copyable `![Description](image:<name>)` snippet\n- [ ] Only real PNG, JPEG, or WebP files are accepted, checked by content with the extension and declared type agreeing; SVG is rejected; the filename is sanitized before use in any path\n- [ ] Images over 5 MB, or beyond 10 on one Data Bite, are rejected with a clear error\n- [ ] Uploading a name the Data Bite already has replaces that image; an image can be deleted\n- [ ] `image:<name>` renders as an `<img>` with the right `src` and the Markdown description as `alt`, in the preview, on the public page, and in the export; an unknown name renders nothing\n- [ ] The edit page warns, without blocking the save, when an `image:` reference has an empty description\n- [ ] A published Data Bite's images are served at `images/data-bites/<slug>/<name>` and exported there with relative paths; a draft's never are, and unpublishing takes them down\n- [ ] Deleting a Data Bite deletes its images\n- [ ] Every image route requires login, carries CSRF on its forms, and rejects a CSRF-stripped post\n- [ ] Tests cover all of the above, including that the export stays identical to the live site (T08)\n\n## Blocked by\n\nNone. Can start immediately.","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"914e91a8-9a6a-45f5-8623-c8850a814006","timestamp":"2026-10-06T03:55:18.564Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"## Parent\n\nPart of #11\n\n## What to build\n\nA Data Bite can carry chart images (PNG, JPEG, WebP), uploaded and deleted on its edit page, and shown inline wherever its Markdown body references them as `image:<name>`: the admin preview, its public page, and the `cms publish` export. This ticket builds the shared image code (validation, storage, the `image:` rewrite in the Markdown pipeline, the public and export paths) that T11 reuses for Reports.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] Any Analyst or the Director can upload an image to any Data Bite, draft or published; the edit page lists its images, each with a copyable `![Description](image:<name>)` snippet\n- [ ] Only real PNG, JPEG, or WebP files are accepted, checked by content with the extension and declared type agreeing; SVG is rejected; the filename is sanitized before use in any path\n- [ ] Images over 5 MB, or beyond 10 on one Data Bite, are rejected with a clear error\n- [ ] Uploading a name the Data Bite already has replaces that image; an image can be deleted\n- [ ] `image:<name>` renders as an `<img>` with the right `src` and the Markdown description as `alt`, in the preview, on the public page, and in the export; an unknown name renders nothing\n- [ ] The edit page warns, without blocking the save, when an `image:` reference has an empty description\n- [ ] A published Data Bite's images are served at `images/data-bites/<slug>/<name>` and exported there with relative paths; a draft's never are, and unpublishing takes them down\n- [ ] Deleting a Data Bite deletes its images\n- [ ] Every image route requires login, carries CSRF on its forms, and rejects a CSRF-stripped post\n- [ ] Tests cover all of the above, including that the export stays identical to the live site (T08)\n\n## Blocked by\n\nNone. {"parentUuid":"6cd52bb5-244d-4689-bce2-9cceeb177718","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"e6020ba8-b21b-4569-a9d2-80edf9fbf144","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"<bash-input>gh issue view 12 --comments</bash-input>"},"uuid":"9ce61667-105d-474f-9b88-d173112c5d1f","timestamp":"2026-10-06T03:57:19.426Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"ac996459-8cb0-425f-b2f6-d5e77314800b","version":"2.1.285","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"9ce61667-105d-474f-9b88-d173112c5d1f","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"e6020ba8-b21b-4569-a9d2-80edf9fbf144","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"<bash-stdout>author:\tDavelleAT\nassociation:\towner\nedited:\tfalse\nstatus:\tnone\n--\n## /code-review: T10 (Standards and Spec reviews, working tree against 9577cac)\n\n### Standards\nThe reviewer found no hard violations. It confirmed relative public paths, CSRF on all three new POSTs and both forms, the `image:` rewrite running before `nh3`, drafts kept out of `site/` (everything goes through `list_published` and `get_published`), no new dependency, and scoped CSS classes.\n\n| Finding (judgement call) | Resolution |\n|---|---|\n| The nosniff `FileResponse` for images appeared twice (admin and public) | **Fixed:** there is now one `chart_images.response(path)` |\n| Admin handler named `image`, where its neighbours are named by action | **Fixed:** renamed to `serve_image` |\n| Max image MB computed inline; `routes/reports.py` uses a module constant | **Fixed:** added `_MAX_IMAGE_MB` |\n| Undefined was guarded twice (the template's `default(none)` and `or None`) | **Fixed:** only the template's guard remains |\n| The `uploads.py` docstring said \"attachment\", which the glossary lists under Avoid | **Fixed:** now \"attached file\" |\n| Test import order | **Fixed** |\n| Bare \"image\" in code rather than \"Chart image\" | **Kept.** The module is `chart_images`, so its functions read as chart images. \"Image\" isn't on the Avoid list. |\n| `\"data-bites\"` kind string in several places; `KINDS` includes `reports` | **Kept.** The public path and the storage folder use the same word on purpose, and T11 (#13) uses `reports` next. |\n| `remove_image` bumps `updated_at` outside its unlink | **Kept.** This matches `reports.remove_file`: the file is removed after the row commits. |\n| The image map is built twice in `public_site.py`; `chart_images.MIB` re-exports `uploads.MIB` | **Kept.** Both are small, and extracting them wouldn't be clearer. |\n\n### Spec\nEvery acceptance criterion has matching code and a test. Nothing is missing.\n\n| Finding | Resolution |\n|---|---|\n| **(c) Id reuse:** `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY` ids can be reused. If a deleted Data Bite's image folder survived (`rmtree(ignore_errors=True)`), a new Data Bite would inherit, and later publish, its images. | **Fixed:** `data_bites.create` clears any leftover folder for its id in the same transaction, and `remove_all` no longer swallows errors. New test: `test_a_new_data_bite_never_inherits_images_left_under_its_id`. |\n| **(c) Export vs crawl:** an uploaded image the body doesn't place is exported, but no crawl reaches it, so T10's parity test allows that one extra file | **Kept, and recorded in ADR-004.** The spec gates per item (\"an image is public only while its item is published\"), so every image on a published item is public, placed or not. The extra file is still byte-identical to what the app serves at that path, and the test checks this. |\n| **T11 reuse:** the empty-description warning was inline in `data_bite_edit.html` | **Fixed:** moved to `admin/_undescribed_images.html`, which sits beside `admin/_chart_images.html` for T11 |\n| (b) `ContentTable.touch`: an image change moves the item's \"Last updated\" | **Kept.** Changing a chart changes the item. T11 needs the same guarded write for the Report lockdown. |\n| (b) New `/admin/data-bites/{id}/preview` route, `.content-body img` CSS, nosniff | **Kept.** Each supports a stated requirement (the preview shows images, images fit the column, no sniffing). |\n\nTests: 46 in `tests/test_t10_chart_images.py`; 235 across the app suites (T00–T10). The 14 failures in `test_ctx_guard.py` and `test_usage_report.py` are the course exercise stubs and were already failing before this change. Also checked in the running app: upload, SVG rejected, draft image 404, publish, public page shows the chart, admin live preview shows it.\n\n--\nauthor:\tDavelleAT\nassociation:\towner\nedited:\tfalse\nstatus:\tnone\n--\nImage visualization is one of the easiest ways one can grasp data so this was a must. This came after because I wanted the core functionalities of the cms to work first.\n--</bash-stdout><bash-stderr></bash-stderr>"},"uuid":"d733a752-b861-438e-8188-f9d7352c88f2","timestamp":"2026-10-06T03:57:20.421Z","turnOrigin":"human","turnPosition":{"promptIndex":5,"turnIndex":5},"userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"ac996459-8cb0-425f-b2f6-d5e77314800b","version":"2.1.285","gitBranch":"main"}
